                                        3. Final query

                                        Up to 30 rows.

                                        4. Columns it returns

                                        ColumnMeaning
                                        workspaceThe workspace, by name: for a table or volume, the home workspace of its catalog.
                                        nameThe workspace, table, person or volume ranked.
                                        kindPerson or Service principal; for a table, Table or View.
                                        directionReads or Writes; on lineage, Upstream or Downstream.
                                        eventsLineage events.
                                        SQL with comments (138 lines)
                                        -- The most active people. Dates from the Time Window; last seen over a year.
-- Workspace id to name.
WITH wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Audit events over the last year, per workspace and identity, with successful logins marked.
aev AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(COALESCE(user_identity.email, user_identity.subject_name)) AS who,
         event_date, event_time,
         service_name = 'accounts' AND action_name RLIKE '(?i)login' AND response.status_code = 200 AS is_login
  FROM system.access.audit
  WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 365) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
),
-- Per workspace and identity: last seen over the year, logins in the window.
seen AS (
  -- last seen over the year; logins in the window
  SELECT workspace_id, who, MAX(event_time) AS last_seen, COUNT_IF(is_login AND event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS logins,
         MAX(CASE WHEN event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS seen_in_window
  FROM aev GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Queries per workspace and identity in the window.
qh AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(executed_by) AS who, COUNT(*) AS queries, MAX(start_time) AS last_query
  FROM system.query.history WHERE start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Table reads (source) and writes (target) in the window, per workspace and identity, from lineage.
lin AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, source_table_full_name AS full_name, 1 AS is_read, 0 AS is_write,
         lower(created_by) AS who
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND source_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING), target_table_full_name, 0, 1, lower(created_by)
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND target_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
),
-- Tables anyone read in the window.
read_any AS (SELECT DISTINCT full_name FROM lin WHERE is_read = 1),
-- Sensitive-looking columns: a column tag, else the column name.
found AS (
  SELECT catalog_name AS c, schema_name AS s, table_name AS t, column_name,
         COALESCE(NULLIF(tag_value, ''), tag_name) AS pii_type, 1 AS rnk
  FROM system.information_schema.column_tags
  WHERE lower(tag_name) RLIKE '^(pii|sensitive|gdpr|class[.])' AND catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  UNION ALL
  SELECT table_catalog, table_schema, table_name, column_name,
         CASE
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'e_?mail' THEN 'Email'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'phone|mobile|msisdn' THEN 'Phone number'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'iban|(^|_)bic($|_)|account_?(no|number)' THEN 'IBAN / bank account'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'ssn|(^|_)ahv|passport|national_?id' THEN 'National ID'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE '(^|_)ip(_?address)?($|_)' THEN 'IP address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'address|street|(^|_)zip|postal' THEN 'Postal address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'birth|(^|_)dob($|_)' THEN 'Date of birth'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'card_?(no|number|num)|(^|_)pan$' THEN 'Payment card'
         END, 2
  FROM system.information_schema.columns
  WHERE table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal') AND table_schema <> 'information_schema'
),
-- One row per sensitive column, and whether a mask covers it.
sens_col AS (
  SELECT f.c || '.' || f.s || '.' || f.t AS full_name, f.column_name, f.pii_type, cm.mask_name IS NOT NULL AS masked
  FROM found f
  LEFT JOIN system.information_schema.column_masks cm
    ON cm.table_catalog = f.c AND cm.table_schema = f.s AND cm.table_name = f.t AND cm.column_name = f.column_name
  WHERE f.pii_type IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY f.c, f.s, f.t, f.column_name ORDER BY f.rnk) = 1
),
-- Per table: its sensitive columns.
sens AS (
  SELECT full_name, COUNT(*) AS sensitive_columns, COUNT_IF(NOT masked) AS unmasked_columns,
         concat_ws(', ', sort_array(collect_set(pii_type))) AS sensitive_types
  FROM sens_col GROUP BY 1
),
-- Per workspace and identity: tables read and written, writes nobody reads, sensitive tables touched.
tw AS (
  SELECT l.workspace_id, l.who,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_read = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_read,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_written,
         SUM(l.is_read) AS reads, SUM(l.is_write) AS writes,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 AND r.full_name IS NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS writes_nobody_reads,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS sensitive_tables
  FROM lin l
  LEFT JOIN read_any r ON r.full_name = l.full_name
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = l.full_name
  GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Per grantee: tables it can read through a direct SELECT or ALL PRIVILEGES grant, and how many are sensitive.
grants AS (
  -- direct grants on tables, per grantee; through a schema, catalog or group isn't expanded
  SELECT lower(p.grantee) AS who, COUNT(DISTINCT p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name) AS direct_grants,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name END) AS sensitive_grants
  FROM system.information_schema.table_privileges p
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name
  WHERE p.privilege_type IN ('SELECT', 'ALL PRIVILEGES') AND p.table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY 1
),
-- Every workspace and identity seen in any of them.
ids AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, who FROM seen UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM qh UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM tw
),
-- All of it together, with the kind: a person (email) or a service principal (UUID).
k AS (
  SELECT i.workspace_id, i.who, CASE WHEN i.who RLIKE '^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$' THEN 'Service principal' WHEN i.who LIKE '%@%' THEN 'Person' ELSE 'Other' END AS kind,
         COALESCE(s.logins, 0) AS logins, COALESCE(q.queries, 0) AS queries,
         COALESCE(w.tables_read, 0) AS tables_read, COALESCE(w.tables_written, 0) AS tables_written,
         COALESCE(w.reads, 0) AS reads, COALESCE(w.writes, 0) AS writes,
         COALESCE(w.writes_nobody_reads, 0) AS writes_nobody_reads, COALESCE(w.sensitive_tables, 0) AS sensitive_tables,
         COALESCE(g.direct_grants, 0) AS direct_grants, COALESCE(g.sensitive_grants, 0) AS sensitive_grants,
         GREATEST(s.last_seen, q.last_query) AS last_seen,
         COALESCE(s.seen_in_window, 0) + CASE WHEN q.queries > 0 OR w.reads + w.writes > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS seen_in_window
  FROM ids i
  LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND s.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN qh q ON q.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND q.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN tw w ON w.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND w.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN grants g ON g.who = i.who
),
-- Per row: last seen band, inactive when not seen for 90+ days, active when seen in the window.
people AS (
  SELECT COALESCE(n.workspace_name, k.workspace_id) AS workspace, k.who AS identity, k.kind, k.logins, k.queries,
         k.tables_read, k.tables_written, k.reads, k.writes, k.writes_nobody_reads, k.sensitive_tables,
         k.direct_grants, k.sensitive_grants,
         CAST(date_format(k.last_seen, 'yyyy-MM-dd') AS STRING) AS last_seen,
         datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) AS days_since,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 30 THEN 'Under 30 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 90 THEN '30-90 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 180 THEN '90-180 days'
              ELSE '180+ days' END AS last_seen_band,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) >= 90 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS inactive,
         CASE WHEN k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active,
         CASE WHEN k.sensitive_tables > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS touched_sensitive,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Person' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_person,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Service principal' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_sp,
         1 AS identities
  FROM k
  LEFT JOIN wsl n ON n.workspace_id = k.workspace_id
  WHERE k.kind <> 'Other' AND k.workspace_id IS NOT NULL AND k.workspace_id <> '0'
),
-- Every row of wd_people.
b AS (
SELECT workspace, identity, kind, logins, queries, tables_read, tables_written, reads, writes, writes_nobody_reads,
       sensitive_tables, direct_grants, sensitive_grants, last_seen, days_since, last_seen_band, inactive, active,
       touched_sensitive, active_person, active_sp, identities
FROM people
ORDER BY writes DESC, reads DESC, queries DESC, identity
),
-- Ranked by read plus write events.
r AS (
  SELECT *, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY reads + writes DESC, identity) AS rnk FROM b WHERE reads + writes > 0
)
SELECT workspace, identity AS name, kind, 'Reads' AS direction, reads AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 15
UNION ALL
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT workspace, identity AS name, kind, 'Writes' AS direction, writes AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 15

                                        Each workspace's most active people

                                        Shows: The 10 with the most read and write events in the window in each workspace, from wd_people: one row per direction. Used by: The most active charts.

                                        Why it is built this way: The same inside one workspace.

                                        How its tables join

                                        Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

                                          1. System tables it reads

                                          • system.access.audit
                                          • system.access.table_lineage
                                          • system.access.workspaces_latest
                                          • system.information_schema.column_masks
                                          • system.information_schema.column_tags
                                          • system.information_schema.columns
                                          • system.information_schema.table_privileges
                                          • system.query.history

                                          2. Steps (CTEs), in order

                                          1. wsl: Workspace id to name.
                                            Its SQL (5 lines)
                                            -- Each workspace's most active people. Dates from the Time Window; last seen over a year.
-- Workspace id to name.
wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
)
                                          2. aev: Audit events over the last year, per workspace and identity, with successful logins marked.
                                            Its SQL (8 lines)
                                            -- Audit events over the last year, per workspace and identity, with successful logins marked.
aev AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(COALESCE(user_identity.email, user_identity.subject_name)) AS who,
         event_date, event_time,
         service_name = 'accounts' AND action_name RLIKE '(?i)login' AND response.status_code = 200 AS is_login
  FROM system.access.audit
  WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 365) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
)
                                          3. seen: Per workspace and identity: last seen over the year, logins in the window.
                                            Its SQL (7 lines)
                                            -- Per workspace and identity: last seen over the year, logins in the window.
seen AS (
  -- last seen over the year; logins in the window
  SELECT workspace_id, who, MAX(event_time) AS last_seen, COUNT_IF(is_login AND event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS logins,
         MAX(CASE WHEN event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS seen_in_window
  FROM aev GROUP BY 1, 2
)
                                          4. qh: Queries per workspace and identity in the window.
                                            Its SQL (6 lines)
                                            -- Queries per workspace and identity in the window.
qh AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(executed_by) AS who, COUNT(*) AS queries, MAX(start_time) AS last_query
  FROM system.query.history WHERE start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  GROUP BY 1, 2
)
                                          5. lin: Table reads (source) and writes (target) in the window, per workspace and identity, from lineage.
                                            Its SQL (11 lines)
                                            -- Table reads (source) and writes (target) in the window, per workspace and identity, from lineage.
lin AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, source_table_full_name AS full_name, 1 AS is_read, 0 AS is_write,
         lower(created_by) AS who
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND source_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING), target_table_full_name, 0, 1, lower(created_by)
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND target_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
)
                                          6. read_any: Tables anyone read in the window.
                                            Its SQL (2 lines)
                                            -- Tables anyone read in the window.
read_any AS (SELECT DISTINCT full_name FROM lin WHERE is_read = 1)
                                          7. found: Sensitive-looking columns: a column tag, else the column name.
                                            Its SQL (21 lines)
                                            -- Sensitive-looking columns: a column tag, else the column name.
found AS (
  SELECT catalog_name AS c, schema_name AS s, table_name AS t, column_name,
         COALESCE(NULLIF(tag_value, ''), tag_name) AS pii_type, 1 AS rnk
  FROM system.information_schema.column_tags
  WHERE lower(tag_name) RLIKE '^(pii|sensitive|gdpr|class[.])' AND catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  UNION ALL
  SELECT table_catalog, table_schema, table_name, column_name,
         CASE
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'e_?mail' THEN 'Email'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'phone|mobile|msisdn' THEN 'Phone number'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'iban|(^|_)bic($|_)|account_?(no|number)' THEN 'IBAN / bank account'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'ssn|(^|_)ahv|passport|national_?id' THEN 'National ID'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE '(^|_)ip(_?address)?($|_)' THEN 'IP address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'address|street|(^|_)zip|postal' THEN 'Postal address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'birth|(^|_)dob($|_)' THEN 'Date of birth'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'card_?(no|number|num)|(^|_)pan$' THEN 'Payment card'
         END, 2
  FROM system.information_schema.columns
  WHERE table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal') AND table_schema <> 'information_schema'
)
                                          8. sens_col: One row per sensitive column, and whether a mask covers it.
                                            Its SQL (9 lines)
                                            -- One row per sensitive column, and whether a mask covers it.
sens_col AS (
  SELECT f.c || '.' || f.s || '.' || f.t AS full_name, f.column_name, f.pii_type, cm.mask_name IS NOT NULL AS masked
  FROM found f
  LEFT JOIN system.information_schema.column_masks cm
    ON cm.table_catalog = f.c AND cm.table_schema = f.s AND cm.table_name = f.t AND cm.column_name = f.column_name
  WHERE f.pii_type IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY f.c, f.s, f.t, f.column_name ORDER BY f.rnk) = 1
)
                                          9. sens: Per table: its sensitive columns.
                                            Its SQL (6 lines)
                                            -- Per table: its sensitive columns.
sens AS (
  SELECT full_name, COUNT(*) AS sensitive_columns, COUNT_IF(NOT masked) AS unmasked_columns,
         concat_ws(', ', sort_array(collect_set(pii_type))) AS sensitive_types
  FROM sens_col GROUP BY 1
)
                                          10. tw: Per workspace and identity: tables read and written, writes nobody reads, sensitive tables touched.
                                            Its SQL (13 lines)
                                            -- Per workspace and identity: tables read and written, writes nobody reads, sensitive tables touched.
tw AS (
  SELECT l.workspace_id, l.who,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_read = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_read,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_written,
         SUM(l.is_read) AS reads, SUM(l.is_write) AS writes,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 AND r.full_name IS NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS writes_nobody_reads,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS sensitive_tables
  FROM lin l
  LEFT JOIN read_any r ON r.full_name = l.full_name
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = l.full_name
  GROUP BY 1, 2
)
                                          11. grants: Per grantee: tables it can read through a direct SELECT or ALL PRIVILEGES grant, and how many are sensitive.
                                            Its SQL (10 lines)
                                            -- Per grantee: tables it can read through a direct SELECT or ALL PRIVILEGES grant, and how many are sensitive.
grants AS (
  -- direct grants on tables, per grantee; through a schema, catalog or group isn't expanded
  SELECT lower(p.grantee) AS who, COUNT(DISTINCT p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name) AS direct_grants,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name END) AS sensitive_grants
  FROM system.information_schema.table_privileges p
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name
  WHERE p.privilege_type IN ('SELECT', 'ALL PRIVILEGES') AND p.table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY 1
)
                                          12. ids: Every workspace and identity seen in any of them.
                                            Its SQL (4 lines)
                                            -- Every workspace and identity seen in any of them.
ids AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, who FROM seen UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM qh UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM tw
)
                                          13. k: All of it together, with the kind: a person (email) or a service principal (UUID).
                                            Its SQL (16 lines)
                                            -- All of it together, with the kind: a person (email) or a service principal (UUID).
k AS (
  SELECT i.workspace_id, i.who, CASE WHEN i.who RLIKE '^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$' THEN 'Service principal' WHEN i.who LIKE '%@%' THEN 'Person' ELSE 'Other' END AS kind,
         COALESCE(s.logins, 0) AS logins, COALESCE(q.queries, 0) AS queries,
         COALESCE(w.tables_read, 0) AS tables_read, COALESCE(w.tables_written, 0) AS tables_written,
         COALESCE(w.reads, 0) AS reads, COALESCE(w.writes, 0) AS writes,
         COALESCE(w.writes_nobody_reads, 0) AS writes_nobody_reads, COALESCE(w.sensitive_tables, 0) AS sensitive_tables,
         COALESCE(g.direct_grants, 0) AS direct_grants, COALESCE(g.sensitive_grants, 0) AS sensitive_grants,
         GREATEST(s.last_seen, q.last_query) AS last_seen,
         COALESCE(s.seen_in_window, 0) + CASE WHEN q.queries > 0 OR w.reads + w.writes > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS seen_in_window
  FROM ids i
  LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND s.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN qh q ON q.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND q.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN tw w ON w.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND w.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN grants g ON g.who = i.who
)
                                          14. people: Per row: last seen band, inactive when not seen for 90+ days, active when seen in the window.
                                            Its SQL (21 lines)
                                            -- Per row: last seen band, inactive when not seen for 90+ days, active when seen in the window.
people AS (
  SELECT COALESCE(n.workspace_name, k.workspace_id) AS workspace, k.who AS identity, k.kind, k.logins, k.queries,
         k.tables_read, k.tables_written, k.reads, k.writes, k.writes_nobody_reads, k.sensitive_tables,
         k.direct_grants, k.sensitive_grants,
         CAST(date_format(k.last_seen, 'yyyy-MM-dd') AS STRING) AS last_seen,
         datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) AS days_since,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 30 THEN 'Under 30 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 90 THEN '30-90 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 180 THEN '90-180 days'
              ELSE '180+ days' END AS last_seen_band,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) >= 90 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS inactive,
         CASE WHEN k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active,
         CASE WHEN k.sensitive_tables > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS touched_sensitive,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Person' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_person,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Service principal' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_sp,
         1 AS identities
  FROM k
  LEFT JOIN wsl n ON n.workspace_id = k.workspace_id
  WHERE k.kind <> 'Other' AND k.workspace_id IS NOT NULL AND k.workspace_id <> '0'
)
                                          15. b: Every row of wd_people.
                                            Its SQL (8 lines)
                                            -- Every row of wd_people.
b AS (
SELECT workspace, identity, kind, logins, queries, tables_read, tables_written, reads, writes, writes_nobody_reads,
       sensitive_tables, direct_grants, sensitive_grants, last_seen, days_since, last_seen_band, inactive, active,
       touched_sensitive, active_person, active_sp, identities
FROM people
ORDER BY writes DESC, reads DESC, queries DESC, identity
)
                                          16. r: Ranked by read plus write events, within each workspace.
                                            Its SQL (6 lines)
                                            -- Ranked by read plus write events, within each workspace.
r AS (
  SELECT *, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace ORDER BY reads + writes DESC, identity) AS rnk FROM b WHERE reads + writes > 0
)
SELECT workspace, identity AS name, kind, 'Reads' AS direction, reads AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 10
UNION ALL

                                          3. Final query

                                          Up to 20 rows per workspace.

                                          4. Columns it returns

                                          ColumnMeaning
                                          workspaceThe workspace, by name: for a table or volume, the home workspace of its catalog.
                                          nameThe workspace, table, person or volume ranked.
                                          kindPerson or Service principal; for a table, Table or View.
                                          directionReads or Writes; on lineage, Upstream or Downstream.
                                          eventsLineage events.
                                          SQL with comments (138 lines)
                                          -- Each workspace's most active people. Dates from the Time Window; last seen over a year.
-- Workspace id to name.
WITH wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Audit events over the last year, per workspace and identity, with successful logins marked.
aev AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(COALESCE(user_identity.email, user_identity.subject_name)) AS who,
         event_date, event_time,
         service_name = 'accounts' AND action_name RLIKE '(?i)login' AND response.status_code = 200 AS is_login
  FROM system.access.audit
  WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 365) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
),
-- Per workspace and identity: last seen over the year, logins in the window.
seen AS (
  -- last seen over the year; logins in the window
  SELECT workspace_id, who, MAX(event_time) AS last_seen, COUNT_IF(is_login AND event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS logins,
         MAX(CASE WHEN event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS seen_in_window
  FROM aev GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Queries per workspace and identity in the window.
qh AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, lower(executed_by) AS who, COUNT(*) AS queries, MAX(start_time) AS last_query
  FROM system.query.history WHERE start_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND start_time < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Table reads (source) and writes (target) in the window, per workspace and identity, from lineage.
lin AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, source_table_full_name AS full_name, 1 AS is_read, 0 AS is_write,
         lower(created_by) AS who
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND source_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING), target_table_full_name, 0, 1, lower(created_by)
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND target_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
),
-- Tables anyone read in the window.
read_any AS (SELECT DISTINCT full_name FROM lin WHERE is_read = 1),
-- Sensitive-looking columns: a column tag, else the column name.
found AS (
  SELECT catalog_name AS c, schema_name AS s, table_name AS t, column_name,
         COALESCE(NULLIF(tag_value, ''), tag_name) AS pii_type, 1 AS rnk
  FROM system.information_schema.column_tags
  WHERE lower(tag_name) RLIKE '^(pii|sensitive|gdpr|class[.])' AND catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  UNION ALL
  SELECT table_catalog, table_schema, table_name, column_name,
         CASE
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'e_?mail' THEN 'Email'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'phone|mobile|msisdn' THEN 'Phone number'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'iban|(^|_)bic($|_)|account_?(no|number)' THEN 'IBAN / bank account'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'ssn|(^|_)ahv|passport|national_?id' THEN 'National ID'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE '(^|_)ip(_?address)?($|_)' THEN 'IP address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'address|street|(^|_)zip|postal' THEN 'Postal address'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'birth|(^|_)dob($|_)' THEN 'Date of birth'
         WHEN lower(column_name) RLIKE 'card_?(no|number|num)|(^|_)pan$' THEN 'Payment card'
         END, 2
  FROM system.information_schema.columns
  WHERE table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal') AND table_schema <> 'information_schema'
),
-- One row per sensitive column, and whether a mask covers it.
sens_col AS (
  SELECT f.c || '.' || f.s || '.' || f.t AS full_name, f.column_name, f.pii_type, cm.mask_name IS NOT NULL AS masked
  FROM found f
  LEFT JOIN system.information_schema.column_masks cm
    ON cm.table_catalog = f.c AND cm.table_schema = f.s AND cm.table_name = f.t AND cm.column_name = f.column_name
  WHERE f.pii_type IS NOT NULL
  QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY f.c, f.s, f.t, f.column_name ORDER BY f.rnk) = 1
),
-- Per table: its sensitive columns.
sens AS (
  SELECT full_name, COUNT(*) AS sensitive_columns, COUNT_IF(NOT masked) AS unmasked_columns,
         concat_ws(', ', sort_array(collect_set(pii_type))) AS sensitive_types
  FROM sens_col GROUP BY 1
),
-- Per workspace and identity: tables read and written, writes nobody reads, sensitive tables touched.
tw AS (
  SELECT l.workspace_id, l.who,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_read = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_read,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 THEN l.full_name END) AS tables_written,
         SUM(l.is_read) AS reads, SUM(l.is_write) AS writes,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN l.is_write = 1 AND r.full_name IS NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS writes_nobody_reads,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN l.full_name END) AS sensitive_tables
  FROM lin l
  LEFT JOIN read_any r ON r.full_name = l.full_name
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = l.full_name
  GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Per grantee: tables it can read through a direct SELECT or ALL PRIVILEGES grant, and how many are sensitive.
grants AS (
  -- direct grants on tables, per grantee; through a schema, catalog or group isn't expanded
  SELECT lower(p.grantee) AS who, COUNT(DISTINCT p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name) AS direct_grants,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN se.full_name IS NOT NULL THEN p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name END) AS sensitive_grants
  FROM system.information_schema.table_privileges p
  LEFT JOIN sens se ON se.full_name = p.table_catalog || '.' || p.table_schema || '.' || p.table_name
  WHERE p.privilege_type IN ('SELECT', 'ALL PRIVILEGES') AND p.table_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY 1
),
-- Every workspace and identity seen in any of them.
ids AS (
  SELECT workspace_id, who FROM seen UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM qh UNION SELECT workspace_id, who FROM tw
),
-- All of it together, with the kind: a person (email) or a service principal (UUID).
k AS (
  SELECT i.workspace_id, i.who, CASE WHEN i.who RLIKE '^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$' THEN 'Service principal' WHEN i.who LIKE '%@%' THEN 'Person' ELSE 'Other' END AS kind,
         COALESCE(s.logins, 0) AS logins, COALESCE(q.queries, 0) AS queries,
         COALESCE(w.tables_read, 0) AS tables_read, COALESCE(w.tables_written, 0) AS tables_written,
         COALESCE(w.reads, 0) AS reads, COALESCE(w.writes, 0) AS writes,
         COALESCE(w.writes_nobody_reads, 0) AS writes_nobody_reads, COALESCE(w.sensitive_tables, 0) AS sensitive_tables,
         COALESCE(g.direct_grants, 0) AS direct_grants, COALESCE(g.sensitive_grants, 0) AS sensitive_grants,
         GREATEST(s.last_seen, q.last_query) AS last_seen,
         COALESCE(s.seen_in_window, 0) + CASE WHEN q.queries > 0 OR w.reads + w.writes > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS seen_in_window
  FROM ids i
  LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND s.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN qh q ON q.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND q.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN tw w ON w.workspace_id = i.workspace_id AND w.who = i.who
  LEFT JOIN grants g ON g.who = i.who
),
-- Per row: last seen band, inactive when not seen for 90+ days, active when seen in the window.
people AS (
  SELECT COALESCE(n.workspace_name, k.workspace_id) AS workspace, k.who AS identity, k.kind, k.logins, k.queries,
         k.tables_read, k.tables_written, k.reads, k.writes, k.writes_nobody_reads, k.sensitive_tables,
         k.direct_grants, k.sensitive_grants,
         CAST(date_format(k.last_seen, 'yyyy-MM-dd') AS STRING) AS last_seen,
         datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) AS days_since,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 30 THEN 'Under 30 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 90 THEN '30-90 days'
              WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) < 180 THEN '90-180 days'
              ELSE '180+ days' END AS last_seen_band,
         CASE WHEN datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(k.last_seen AS DATE)) >= 90 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS inactive,
         CASE WHEN k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active,
         CASE WHEN k.sensitive_tables > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS touched_sensitive,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Person' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_person,
         CASE WHEN k.kind = 'Service principal' AND k.seen_in_window > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END AS active_sp,
         1 AS identities
  FROM k
  LEFT JOIN wsl n ON n.workspace_id = k.workspace_id
  WHERE k.kind <> 'Other' AND k.workspace_id IS NOT NULL AND k.workspace_id <> '0'
),
-- Every row of wd_people.
b AS (
SELECT workspace, identity, kind, logins, queries, tables_read, tables_written, reads, writes, writes_nobody_reads,
       sensitive_tables, direct_grants, sensitive_grants, last_seen, days_since, last_seen_band, inactive, active,
       touched_sensitive, active_person, active_sp, identities
FROM people
ORDER BY writes DESC, reads DESC, queries DESC, identity
),
-- Ranked by read plus write events, within each workspace.
r AS (
  SELECT *, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace ORDER BY reads + writes DESC, identity) AS rnk FROM b WHERE reads + writes > 0
)
SELECT workspace, identity AS name, kind, 'Reads' AS direction, reads AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 10
UNION ALL
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT workspace, identity AS name, kind, 'Writes' AS direction, writes AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 10

                                          Each workspace's most active volumes

                                          Shows: The 10 with the most read and write events in the window in each workspace, from wd_volumes: one row per direction. Used by: The most active charts.

                                          Why it is built this way: The volumes in daily use inside one workspace; the rest are clean-up candidates.

                                          How its tables join

                                          Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

                                            1. System tables it reads

                                            • system.access.audit
                                            • system.access.table_lineage
                                            • system.access.workspaces_latest
                                            • system.information_schema.catalogs
                                            • system.information_schema.volumes

                                            2. Steps (CTEs), in order

                                            1. wsl: Workspace id to name and URL.
                                              Its SQL (6 lines)
                                              -- Each workspace's most active volumes. Dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name and URL.
wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name, workspace_url
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
)
                                            2. seen: Lineage reads and writes per catalog and workspace over 90 days.
                                              Its SQL (10 lines)
                                              -- Lineage reads and writes per catalog and workspace over 90 days.
seen AS (
  SELECT catalog, CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, COUNT(*) AS events FROM (
    SELECT workspace_id, source_table_catalog AS catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
    UNION ALL
    SELECT workspace_id, target_table_catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  ) WHERE catalog IS NOT NULL GROUP BY 1, 2
)
                                            3. home: Each catalog's home workspace: the one with the most of them.
                                              Its SQL (7 lines)
                                              -- Each catalog's home workspace: the one with the most of them.
home AS (
  SELECT c.catalog_name AS catalog, max_by(s.workspace_id, s.events) AS workspace_id
  FROM system.information_schema.catalogs c LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.catalog = c.catalog_name
  WHERE c.catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY c.catalog_name
)
                                            4. place: Each catalog with its home workspace's name and URL.
                                              Its SQL (7 lines)
                                              -- Each catalog with its home workspace's name and URL.
place AS (
  -- a catalog without lineage in 90 days keeps its rows, under No home workspace
  SELECT h.catalog, h.workspace_id, w.workspace_url,
         COALESCE(w.workspace_name, h.workspace_id, 'No home workspace') AS workspace
  FROM home h LEFT JOIN wsl w ON w.workspace_id = h.workspace_id
)
                                            5. v: Every volume, without the system catalogs.
                                              Its SQL (6 lines)
                                              -- Every volume, without the system catalogs.
v AS (
  SELECT volume_catalog AS catalog, volume_schema AS sch, volume_name, volume_type, created,
         volume_catalog || '.' || volume_schema || '.' || volume_name AS full_name
  FROM system.information_schema.volumes WHERE volume_catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
)
                                            6. ev: File-system events under /Volumes over 365 days: who, when, read or write.
                                              Its SQL (10 lines)
                                              -- File-system events under /Volumes over 365 days: who, when, read or write.
ev AS (
  SELECT regexp_extract(COALESCE(request_params['path'], request_params['file_path'], request_params['directory_path']),
                        '^/Volumes/([^/]+)/([^/]+)/([^/]+)', 0) AS path_root,
         lower(COALESCE(user_identity.email, user_identity.subject_name)) AS who, event_time,
         action_name RLIKE '(?i)put|upload|create|delete|write|mkdir' AS is_write
  FROM system.access.audit
  WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 365) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
    AND service_name = 'filesystem' AND response.status_code = 200
)
                                            7. used: Per volume: reads, writes, readers and writers in the window; last read and write.
                                              Its SQL (9 lines)
                                              -- Per volume: reads, writes, readers and writers in the window; last read and write.
used AS (
  SELECT replace(substr(path_root, 10), '/', '.') AS full_name,
         COUNT_IF(NOT is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS reads, COUNT_IF(is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS writes,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN NOT is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN who END) AS readers,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN who END) AS writers,
         MAX(CASE WHEN NOT is_write THEN event_time END) AS last_read, MAX(CASE WHEN is_write THEN event_time END) AS last_write
  FROM ev WHERE path_root <> '' GROUP BY 1
)
                                            8. j: Everything per volume.
                                              Its SQL (10 lines)
                                              -- Everything per volume.
j AS (
  SELECT p.workspace, v.catalog, v.sch AS schema_name, v.full_name, lower(v.volume_type) AS volume_type, v.created,
         vs.files, vs.size_in_bytes, COALESCE(u.reads, 0) AS reads, COALESCE(u.writes, 0) AS writes,
         COALESCE(u.readers, 0) AS readers, COALESCE(u.writers, 0) AS writers, u.last_read, u.last_write
  FROM v
  LEFT JOIN place p ON p.catalog = v.catalog
  LEFT JOIN main.data_usage.volume_sizes vs ON vs.catalog = v.catalog AND vs.schema = v.sch AND vs.volume_name = v.volume_name
  LEFT JOIN used u ON u.full_name = v.full_name
)
                                            9. c: Its usage class, by the same rules as tables.
                                              Its SQL (9 lines)
                                              -- Its usage class, by the same rules as tables.
c AS (
  SELECT *, CASE WHEN writes > 0 AND reads = 0 THEN 'Written, never read'
            WHEN reads = 0 AND writes = 0 AND last_read IS NULL AND last_write IS NULL
                 AND created >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 7) THEN 'Active'
            WHEN GREATEST(last_read, last_write) IS NULL OR datediff(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), CAST(GREATEST(last_read, last_write) AS DATE)) >= 90 THEN 'Idle 90d+'
            WHEN reads = 0 THEN 'Cooling'
            ELSE 'Active' END AS usage_class FROM j
)
                                            10. b: Every row of wd_volumes.
                                              Its SQL (12 lines)
                                              -- Every row of wd_volumes.
b AS (
SELECT workspace, catalog, schema_name, full_name AS volume, volume_type, usage_class, CASE usage_class WHEN 'Written, never read' THEN 1 WHEN 'Idle 90d+' THEN 2 WHEN 'Cooling' THEN 3 ELSE 4 END AS class_rank,
       files, size_in_bytes, ROUND(size_in_bytes / 1073741824.0, 3) AS size_gb,
       reads, writes, readers, writers,
       CAST(date_format(last_read, 'yyyy-MM-dd') AS STRING) AS last_read,
       CAST(date_format(last_write, 'yyyy-MM-dd') AS STRING) AS last_write,
       CASE WHEN usage_class IN ('Written, never read', 'Idle 90d+') THEN ROUND(size_in_bytes / 1073741824.0, 3) ELSE 0 END AS unread_gb,
       1 AS volumes
FROM c
ORDER BY class_rank, size_in_bytes DESC NULLS LAST, volume
)
                                            11. r: Ranked by read plus write events, within each workspace.
                                              Its SQL (6 lines)
                                              -- Ranked by read plus write events, within each workspace.
r AS (
  SELECT *, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace ORDER BY reads + writes DESC, volume) AS rnk FROM b WHERE reads + writes > 0
)
SELECT workspace, volume AS name, usage_class, 'Reads' AS direction, reads AS events FROM r WHERE rnk <= 10
UNION ALL

                                            3. Final query

                                            Up to 20 rows per workspace.

                                            4. Columns it returns

                                            ColumnMeaning
                                            workspaceThe workspace, by name: for a table or volume, the home workspace of its catalog.
                                            nameThe workspace, table, person or volume ranked.
                                            usage_classWritten, never read; Idle 90d+; Cooling; or Active (app/core/usage.py).
                                            directionReads or Writes; on lineage, Upstream or Downstream.
                                            eventsLineage events.
                                            SQL with comments (82 lines)
                                            -- Each workspace's most active volumes. Dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name and URL.
WITH wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name, workspace_url
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),

    UNION ALL

    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  ) WHERE catalog IS NOT NULL GROUP BY 1, 2
),

-- Every volume, without the system catalogs.

         volume_catalog || '.' || volume_schema || '.' || volume_name AS full_name

-- File-system events under /Volumes over 365 days: who, when, read or write.
ev AS (
  SELECT regexp_extract(COALESCE(request_params['path'], request_params['file_path'], request_params['directory_path']),

         COUNT_IF(NOT is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS reads, COUNT_IF(is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE)) AS writes,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN NOT is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN who END) AS readers,
         COUNT(DISTINCT CASE WHEN is_write AND event_time >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) THEN who END) AS writers,

),

                                            Each workspace's busiest tables per day

                                            Shows: wd_daily for the 10 tables of each workspace with the most read and write events in the window. Used by: Busiest tables per day on Workspace detail.

                                            Why it is built this way: Whether a workspace's main tables are used every day or in bursts.

                                            How its tables join

                                            Grey: system tables. Blue border: a shared check (its steps are listed under Steps). White: steps of the query. Green: the rows the dashboard shows. ◆ marks the columns the joins use.

                                              1. System tables it reads

                                              • system.access.table_lineage
                                              • system.access.workspaces_latest
                                              • system.information_schema.catalogs

                                              2. Steps (CTEs), in order

                                              1. wsl: Workspace id to name and URL.
                                                Its SQL (6 lines)
                                                -- Each workspace's busiest tables per day. Dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name and URL.
wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name, workspace_url
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
)
                                              2. seen: Lineage reads and writes per catalog and workspace over 90 days.
                                                Its SQL (10 lines)
                                                -- Lineage reads and writes per catalog and workspace over 90 days.
seen AS (
  SELECT catalog, CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, COUNT(*) AS events FROM (
    SELECT workspace_id, source_table_catalog AS catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
    UNION ALL
    SELECT workspace_id, target_table_catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  ) WHERE catalog IS NOT NULL GROUP BY 1, 2
)
                                              3. home: Each catalog's home workspace: the one with the most of them.
                                                Its SQL (7 lines)
                                                -- Each catalog's home workspace: the one with the most of them.
home AS (
  SELECT c.catalog_name AS catalog, max_by(s.workspace_id, s.events) AS workspace_id
  FROM system.information_schema.catalogs c LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.catalog = c.catalog_name
  WHERE c.catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY c.catalog_name
)
                                              4. place: Each catalog with its home workspace's name and URL.
                                                Its SQL (7 lines)
                                                -- Each catalog with its home workspace's name and URL.
place AS (
  -- a catalog without lineage in 90 days keeps its rows, under No home workspace
  SELECT h.catalog, h.workspace_id, w.workspace_url,
         COALESCE(w.workspace_name, h.workspace_id, 'No home workspace') AS workspace
  FROM home h LEFT JOIN wsl w ON w.workspace_id = h.workspace_id
)
                                              5. b: Every row of wd_daily.
                                                Its SQL (16 lines)
                                                -- Every row of wd_daily.
b AS (
SELECT l.day, p.workspace, l.full_name AS table_name, l.direction, COUNT(*) AS events
FROM (
  SELECT event_date AS day, source_table_full_name AS full_name, source_table_catalog AS catalog, 'Reads' AS direction
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND source_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT event_date, target_table_full_name, target_table_catalog, 'Writes'
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND target_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
) l
LEFT JOIN place p ON p.catalog = l.catalog
WHERE l.catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4
)
                                              6. t: Each workspace's tables ranked by events.
                                                Its SQL (5 lines)
                                                -- Each workspace's tables ranked by events.
t AS (
  SELECT workspace, table_name, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace ORDER BY SUM(events) DESC, table_name) AS rnk
  FROM b GROUP BY workspace, table_name
)

                                              3. Final query

                                              One row per day, table and direction, 10 tables per workspace.

                                              4. Columns it returns

                                              ColumnMeaning
                                              dayThe day.
                                              workspaceThe workspace, by name: for a table or volume, the home workspace of its catalog.
                                              table_namecatalog.schema.table.
                                              directionReads or Writes; on lineage, Upstream or Downstream.
                                              eventsLineage events.
                                              SQL with comments (47 lines)
                                              -- Each workspace's busiest tables per day. Dates from the Time Window.
-- Workspace id to name and URL.
WITH wsl AS (
  SELECT CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, workspace_name, workspace_url
  FROM system.access.workspaces_latest
),
-- Lineage reads and writes per catalog and workspace over 90 days.
seen AS (
  SELECT catalog, CAST(workspace_id AS STRING) AS workspace_id, COUNT(*) AS events FROM (
    SELECT workspace_id, source_table_catalog AS catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
    UNION ALL
    SELECT workspace_id, target_table_catalog FROM system.access.table_lineage
    WHERE event_date >= date_sub(date_add(:time_range.max, 1), 90) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1)
  ) WHERE catalog IS NOT NULL GROUP BY 1, 2
),
-- Each catalog's home workspace: the one with the most of them.
home AS (
  SELECT c.catalog_name AS catalog, max_by(s.workspace_id, s.events) AS workspace_id
  FROM system.information_schema.catalogs c LEFT JOIN seen s ON s.catalog = c.catalog_name
  WHERE c.catalog_name NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
  GROUP BY c.catalog_name
),
-- Each catalog with its home workspace's name and URL.
place AS (
  -- a catalog without lineage in 90 days keeps its rows, under No home workspace
  SELECT h.catalog, h.workspace_id, w.workspace_url,
         COALESCE(w.workspace_name, h.workspace_id, 'No home workspace') AS workspace
  FROM home h LEFT JOIN wsl w ON w.workspace_id = h.workspace_id
),
-- Every row of wd_daily.
b AS (
SELECT l.day, p.workspace, l.full_name AS table_name, l.direction, COUNT(*) AS events
FROM (
  SELECT event_date AS day, source_table_full_name AS full_name, source_table_catalog AS catalog, 'Reads' AS direction
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND source_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT event_date, target_table_full_name, target_table_catalog, 'Writes'
  FROM system.access.table_lineage
  WHERE event_date >= CAST(:time_range.min AS DATE) AND event_date < date_add(:time_range.max, 1) AND target_table_full_name IS NOT NULL
) l
LEFT JOIN place p ON p.catalog = l.catalog
WHERE l.catalog NOT IN ('system', 'samples', '__databricks_internal')
GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4
),
-- Each workspace's tables ranked by events.
t AS (
  SELECT workspace, table_name, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY workspace ORDER BY SUM(events) DESC, table_name) AS rnk
  FROM b GROUP BY workspace, table_name
)
-- Final SELECT: the rows the widgets read.
SELECT b.day, b.workspace, b.table_name, b.direction, b.events
FROM b JOIN t ON t.workspace <=> b.workspace AND t.table_name = b.table_name WHERE t.rnk <= 10
